Host-Level Risk & CVE Correlation

CVE candidates are deduplicated within each host, not across different hosts. Remediation Guidance

Advisory guidance only. ReconForge does not execute changes, does not invent fixed versions, and requires vendor/change-control validation.

192.168.7.128:21 / ftp — Review FTP transport and authentication

medium suggested review Confidence: medium; Traditional FTP does not encrypt credentials or file contents by default.

Evidence: {"host": "192.168.7.128", "identification_source": "Nmap service detection", "port": 21, "product": "vsftpd", "service_name": "ftp", "version": "2.3.4"}

Recommended actions

  • Confirm whether FTP is required; disable it if unused.
  • Prefer SFTP or FTPS with enforced encryption and managed credentials.
  • Restrict access to approved clients and review anonymous access and write permissions.

How to verify

  • Confirm the legacy FTP listener is removed or restricted as intended.
  • Test encrypted file-transfer access and verify anonymous access is disabled unless explicitly required.
  • Re-scan and review service configuration.

192.168.7.128:22 / ssh — Harden SSH administration

medium suggested review Confidence: medium; SSH is encrypted, but outdated software or permissive configuration can still create risk.

Evidence: {"host": "192.168.7.128", "identification_source": "Nmap service detection", "port": 22, "product": "OpenSSH", "service_name": "ssh", "version": "4.7p1 Debian 8ubuntu1"}

Recommended actions

  • Apply supported vendor security updates after compatibility review.
  • Prefer key-based authentication, disable obsolete algorithms, and restrict administrative access.
  • Review privileged accounts, authentication logs, and rate-limiting controls.

How to verify

  • Validate effective SSH configuration and supported cryptographic algorithms.
  • Test access using approved accounts before closing the change.
  • Confirm logs capture successful and failed authentication attempts.
